# Hive — Development TODO

**Source of truth:** `docs/hive.md` (Portuguese). Every task cites the decisions (`#N`, `D#`) it implements; read them before starting the task.
**Visual reference:** `docs/prototype/` (read `docs/prototype/README.md` first).
**Rules:** `CLAUDE.md`.

## How to use this file

1. Work **top to bottom**, one task at a time. Do not start a task whose dependencies are not checked.
2. Before a task: read the cited decisions in `docs/hive.md`. After a task: all quality gates green (CLAUDE.md), then tick the box and add the branch name next to it.
3. **⏸ Checkpoint** = stop and wait for the human. Do not continue past a checkpoint on your own.
4. If a decision is unclear, missing, or contradicted by reality: stop and ask. Never change a decision yourself.
5. Finished stages move word for word to `docs/history/` and keep one line under "Earlier stages". A default a task takes (a choice `docs/hive.md` does not settle, "pending human review") also gets a row in the "Defaults ledger" with status `pending`.

## Defaults ledger

Every default the agents took in Stages 1–9, one row per task (reviewed 2026-09-28: the human delegated the pass to the orchestrator, which accepted every row and the six open questions of the Stage 9 checkpoint as they stand) (a summary: the task's note, here or in `docs/history/stages-0-8.md`, has the full text). The human sets each status to `accepted` or `changed: <what>` in one pass; a changed default becomes a task. Left out: the in-app chat's defaults (7.3a–7.3j, 8.4, 8.7–8.10, 8.13, 8.14), since 9.2 and 9.35 removed the chat and the conversation view.

| 1.5 | The service owns `<data>/hive/projects.json` (atomic, 0600); a corrupt file is moved to `projects.json.corrupt` and the list starts empty; a typed path is normalised to its main worktree; adding a project twice answers the same project; names: a Claude worktree's folder, else its branch; a "Refresh worktrees" button (not in the prototype). | accepted |

| 1.9 | Ctrl+Shift+N targets the selected project, else the selected worktree's project, else the first project, else Add project; "+" opens a terminal in the selected worktree (the prototype's "+" opens the picker); tree keys ↑/↓, ←/→ and Enter (selects; no Enter-focuses-the-terminal). | accepted |
| 1.10 | The close dialog's texts ("Close Hive?", "N agents are running…"); on exit the app closes the bridge's stdin, waits up to 2 s, then kills it. | accepted |

| 2.2 | An agent with no state yet shows as idle; no "time since" (no timestamp in the protocol); the working pulse stops under `prefers-reduced-motion`. | accepted |
| 2.3 | `urgency` 0 (ended) … 6 (waiting for permission); pending = waiting for permission, error or waiting for you; a collapsed project or worktree shows its most urgent state icon (no bell); F8 goes to the next pending agent in tree order, wrapping; no Shift+F8. | accepted |
| 2.4 | The tone: an 880 Hz, 0.2 s Web Audio beep, at most one per 500 ms; an agent's first state and the snapshot after a reconnect are silent; "finished" = working or with subagents → waiting for you, as an OS notification "Agent finished". | accepted |
| 3.1 | One watched worktree at a time (the files panel's); files = `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, at most 50 000 files / 3 MiB of names; at most 8192 inotify watches; re-listed after 200 ms of quiet (1 s at most); every git call with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`. | accepted |
| 3.2 | Untracked files show as "A", renamed as "R" in blue; a folder shows its strongest status (D > A > R > M); git output capped at 16 MiB. (The diff base, `HEAD`, became the human's choice in 9.11.) | accepted |
| 3.3 | Each side of a file capped at 1 MiB (not 2 MiB: both sides fit one frame); not UTF-8 = binary; `version` = byte length + SipHash; a changed file opens as a unified diff with unchanged stretches collapsed; One Dark theme. | accepted |

| 28.10 | `hive agents` exits 3 when no service runs (1 for any other failure, 2 for a usage error as clap gives); `--json` prints one line (`{"agents": [...]}`, `{"worktrees": [...]}`), the agents in terminal order with their live subagents; the text output is tab-separated (terminal, state, worktree else cwd, context %, title, activity; `-` when unknown, control and invisible characters as spaces, no header); the CLI waits 10 s for the answer and reads at most 4096 agents; the service gives a client 5 s to read the list; the request rides a hook-role connection (no new role). | pending |
| 28.19 | Each worktree's history of shown tabs holds its last 20 (bar keys; a plain terminal's channel is dropped between runs, as in the tab order); at most 200 worktrees' places are kept, the least recently changed forgotten (`hive.places`). Closing a tab inside a pair still shows its other pane. Coming back, the place's history is used only when the tab shown is not in its bar; with no history the last terminal shows, as before. The right panel's view is per worktree; a worktree never shown takes the view shown, and a view picked with the move is the new worktree's. The trees' scroll and active row and the Comments and Sessions views' scroll are kept per worktree for this run only (in memory, not `localStorage`); a tree whose active row is gone keeps the same place in the list. | pending |

| 28.21 | A live agent = one the service placed in the worktree whose tab is open on the shown host, its terminal not exited and its state not `ended`, in the user's agent order (8.2). With several agents the buttons always open the menu (the one last picked first), never send straight to the remembered one; the keyboard paths with no menu (Ctrl+Shift+L, the palette's "Send open comments") send to the agent last picked in that worktree while it lives, else the first in the agent order. A menu entry: the state icon, the tab's title (the session's name, else the worktree's), then the state's name and its activity. The pick is kept in `localStorage` (`hive.sendTarget`, worktree → session id, the last 200 worktrees). A terminal picked in the menu whose agent ended meanwhile is refused ("That agent has ended"), never redirected. The file view's button is now "Send to agent"; its and the Comments buttons' tooltips say "the agent". The mock's `title <name>` names a running agent's session (for the e2e). | pending |

## Stage 22 — Claude accounts and the Hive folder

Created 2026-11-02 at the human's request, from a conversation with the human that day. Planned as **day 2** of the plan the human asked for on 2026-11-03 ("releasing everything in one release would be too heavy", then "fewer days, at most 4"): one stage per day, one release each, in this order. Task numbers changed then; each moved task says its old number. Decision ids (`D22.N`) kept theirs.

**Rebalanced 2026-11-04 at the human's request** ("too much in Stage 22; weigh by each task's complexity, not their number, and spread the load"): Stages 22–24 regrouped so each carries about the same work, dependencies kept in order (a task never needs one of a later stage). Each moved task says its old number.

## Stage 23 — Projects, scripts and tables

Created 2026-11-02 at the human's request, from a conversation with the human that day. Planned as **day 3** of the plan the human asked for on 2026-11-03 ("releasing everything in one release would be too heavy", then "fewer days, at most 4"): one stage per day, one release each, in this order. Task numbers changed then; each moved task says its old number. Decision ids (`D22.N`) kept theirs. New dependencies need the human's approval (rule 3) before `cargo add`.

**Rebalanced 2026-11-04 at the human's request** ("too much in Stage 22; weigh by each task's complexity, not their number, and spread the load"): Stages 22–24 regrouped so each carries about the same work, dependencies kept in order (a task never needs one of a later stage). Each moved task says its old number.

Asked by the human 2026-11-03: "I want to create projects from Hive, and open folders even without git; I always have to open the Mac's terminal just to create a folder and run claude in it."

- [x] **23.1 A plain folder as a project.** `task/23.1-folder-project` — *D22.11 as proposed. `projects::add` follows a folder in no repository holding none as a group of none: `Project.folder` (`serde(default)`), kept in `groups` (an old `spaces.json` loads unchanged); one worktree, its folder, where its terminals and agents are placed (space environment, no `HIVE_*`). Refused with the new `ProjectError::ProtectedFolder`: `/`, the home folder itself, Hive's and Claude's folders and what they hold (`Projects::protect`, set in `daemon::run` by `protected`). Files panel: `Projects::tree` accepts its folder (the same path checks), listed (`files::Watcher::plain`) and searched (`search::search`) as the work tree of an empty bare repository `<data>/hive/plain.git` (`git::command_in`); no `changes` or status, `files` after every change, `open_file` without a base (`file::read_plain`). `init_git {id}` (new request; `Projects::init_git`, `git init -q`, separate arguments); the registry's watch of its folder sees the `.git`, `watch_registry` (and `list_projects`) runs `Projects::settle`, which takes it out of `groups`: a project, no re-add; a repository made inside makes it a group (14.1). `State::add_project` is the one entry point that follows a folder, for 23.2. UI: `PlainFolderIcon` row (`.folder`), its new chat and agents; `ProjectMenu` "Initialize git" and "Remove project…"; `panelViews` (Files, Sessions); `panelWorktree` includes it; no `list_changes`; `followOpenFile` re-asks on each listing; mock `MOCK_FOLDER` (`/home/user/projects/notes`), `initGit`, home refused. Tests: Rust unit (`projects`, `files`, `search`, `daemon::protected`), integration (`folders.rs`; `projects.rs` home refused), bun (Sidebar, WorktreeMenu, RightPanel, Palette, follow, mock, tauri), e2e (`folders.e2e.ts`). Defaults in the ledger.* *(Was 22.3, earlier 22.17.)* Today `projects::add` (`projects.rs:281`) refuses a folder in no git repository unless it holds repositories (then a group, 14.1): "… is not in a git repository with a working tree". **D22.11 (the human):** proposal, such a folder is followed as a **folder project**, built on the group's model (14.1): one worktree that is its folder (never a git worktree), its own terminals and chats in it, the space's environment and no `HIVE_*`, in session history like any project; it has a files panel (files read and saved as in a worktree, the same path checks), but no changes, pull requests, runs or new worktree, and its menu offers **"Initialize git"** (`git init` with separate arguments; then the registry's watcher, which already sees a `.git` made, 14.1, turns it into a normal project with no re-add). A repository later cloned into it turns it into a group, as 14.1 does. The untrusted-folder confirmation (#46) treats it as any project. Refused as today: the home folder itself, `/` and the data/config folders of Hive and Claude (a typo should not follow the whole disk). Tests: Rust, a plain folder followed, `git init` turning it into a project, a repository made inside turning it into a group, the refused folders, the old `spaces.json` loading unchanged; integration, a terminal in the folder and its agent under it; bun, the sidebar row, the menu, no changes panel; e2e on the mock transport.
- [x] **23.2 New project from Hive.** `task/23.2-new-project` — *D22.12 as proposed. Protocol: `create_project {parent, name, git}` answered by `project_created {project, path}` (after `spaces`, and `projects` for a folder project) or `add_project_failed` with the new `ProjectError`s `invalid_name`, `name_taken`, `create_failed`; `create_dir {parent, name}` answered by `dir_created` / `create_dir_failed`. Service: `dirs::check_name` (one segment: not empty, at most 255 bytes, no `/`, `\` or `: * ? " < > |` (security review: `D:x` is a path on drive D: on Windows), no leading dot, no control character), `dirs::existing` (the add's parent checks, shared with `projects::validate`), `dirs::target` (parent resolved + name), `dirs::make` (`DirBuilder`, not recursive, 0755 less the umask, `AlreadyExists` is `name_taken`); `Projects::create` refuses a folder 23.1 protects before anything is made, runs `git init -q` when asked (its failure `create_failed`, the folder stays), then `State::follow` (the old `add_project`, shared) follows it. App: the dialog's "Existing folder / New project" choice; the parent is a `FolderField` starting at the last parent used (`hive.newProjectParent` in local storage, else the home folder); on `project_created` it selects the folder, opens `openClaude` in it when asked, and closes. `FolderField`'s `newFolder` button (both modes): a name typed in a row of the list, Enter sends `create_dir`, Esc cancels; made, the field enters it. Tests: Rust unit (name rules, target, make, `create_dir`'s answer, `Projects::create` with/without git, taken, missing parent, protected), integration (`folders.rs`: both kinds, the terminal in it, refusals, a failing `git init`), bun (dialog mode, validation shown, terminal opened, remembered parent, New folder), e2e `e2e/new-project.e2e.ts`.*

Asked by the human 2026-11-03, after setting up a project's scripts (6.8): a run script has to be typed into Settings by hand, with `--port $HIVE_PORT` remembered each time, even though the repository already lists its scripts (`package.json`, a task runner as in Go projects), and the "Run: <name>" items are hidden in the worktree's context menu and the palette. **Human decision 2026-11-03: this replaces the part of `docs/hive.md` #49 that says scripts are "never read from the repository"** ("I don't care what was decided before, the need came up now"); the human updates `docs/hive.md`. What stays from #49: nothing from the repository runs by itself (setup and archive, which run with no click, stay only in Hive's settings).

- [x] **23.3 `PORT` in a worktree's terminals.** `main` — *`scripts::env(root, worktree, port, inherited)` adds `PORT` = the block's first port beside `HIVE_PORT`, unless `inherited` (the service's own `PORT`, `State::hive_env` passes `std::env::var_os("PORT")`) is set and not empty: the process inherits that one. A `PORT` exported by the shell config wins in a terminal anyway (set after). Worktree terminals, run scripts and setup (typed into those terminals) and the archive script; nothing outside a worktree or in a group's folder, nor without a block. The tests' `Env::hive` removes `PORT`. Projects hint names `$PORT` and Vite's `server.port`. Default in the ledger.* *(Was 22.5, earlier 22.20.)* Today a worktree's processes get `HIVE_PORT`, `HIVE_WORKTREE_PATH` and `HIVE_ROOT_PATH` (`scripts::env`, `scripts.rs:111`), so a dev server takes its reserved port only when the command says so (`pnpm dev --port $HIVE_PORT`); appending a flag is not an option, each tool takes the port differently (`--port`, `-p`, `npm run dev -- --port`, Django's `host:port`, a Go app reading `PORT`). **D22.14 (the human):** proposal, `env()` also sets **`PORT`** to the block's first port, the convention Next, Express, Remix, Nuxt, Rails and most Go apps read, so a plain `pnpm dev` lands on the worktree's port; a `PORT` the user's space or shell already sets is not overridden (say which wins and why in the report); the settings' hint under the project's scripts names `PORT` and says Vite needs `server.port: Number(process.env.PORT)` in its config. Only the worktree terminals, run scripts and setup/archive Hive starts; nothing outside a worktree. ## Stage 24 — Files inside the app and the agent's browser

Created 2026-11-02 at the human's request, from a conversation with the human that day. Planned as **day 4** of the plan the human asked for on 2026-11-03 ("releasing everything in one release would be too heavy", then "fewer days, at most 4"): one stage per day, one release each, in this order. Task numbers changed then; each moved task says its old number. Decision ids (`D22.N`) kept theirs. **Changes the central concept** of `docs/hive.md` ("Hive only observes the agents"): the human said it was an MVP limit and may be dropped, so Hive may now give the agents tools (24.5–24.6); the human updates `docs/hive.md`. New dependencies need the human's approval (rule 3) before `cargo add`.

**Rebalanced 2026-11-04 at the human's request** ("too much in Stage 22; weigh by each task's complexity, not their number, and spread the load"): Stages 22–24 regrouped so each carries about the same work, dependencies kept in order (a task never needs one of a later stage). Each moved task says its old number.

- [ ] **24.1 Spike: files and pages inside the app.** *(Was 23.3, earlier 22.2.)* A throwaway branch, nothing merged. In the real app on macOS (and WebView2 if a Windows machine is at hand): a custom URI scheme registered in `src-tauri` (`hive-file://…`, its bytes from the service) shown in (a) an `<img>`, (b) an `<iframe>` with a PDF (does the WebView's own PDF viewer draw it inside an iframe?), (c) a sandboxed `<iframe>` with an HTML file whose CSS and images load through the same scheme, (d) an `<iframe>` of `http://localhost:<port>` (CSP `frame-src`, mixed content, `X-Frame-Options`); plus how the service sends a file larger than one frame (`MAX_PAYLOAD`, 4 MiB). Report what works on each platform. ⏸ Checkpoint: the human picks 24.2–24.4's design from it.
  - **Spike result and the human's choices (2026-11-07, 24.1 checkpoint):** branch `spike/24.1-hive-file` (8b84218, not merged). On macOS: `<img>` (svg included, its script does not run), PDF in a plain iframe (WebKit's viewer; blank when sandboxed, on WebView2 too), a `sandbox="allow-scripts"` HTML page with relative files, and a `localhost` iframe all work; today's navigation guard cancels every iframe and `app_url` / the CSP must admit the scheme and the dev-server ports. Design for 24.2–24.4: URL `hive-file://localhost/<worktree token>/<relative path>` (`http://hive-file.localhost/…` on Windows), the token issued by the service for a followed worktree; the scheme handler (`register_asynchronous_uri_scheme_protocol`, no new dependency) pulls the file in ranges, `ReadFile {id, worktree, path, offset, len ≤ 1 MiB}`, the service checking the path on every read (24.7's no-follow open) and answering with size, type read from the bytes and version, the bytes in a new binary frame (no base64); responses carry the service's type and `nosniff`. **Human:** caps 20 MiB for an image, 100 MiB for a PDF, 20 MiB for an HTML page and each of its files (above: "Open in default app"); the PDF iframe is **not sandboxed** (no `pdf.js`); the HTML preview allows its inline scripts and styles, CSP `default-src 'none'`, the scheme plus `'unsafe-inline'` for script and style, `connect-src 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`; the IPC exposure found is fixed first, as 24.14.

- [x] **24.7 No race between a file's path check and its opening.** `task/24.7-open-nofollow` — *23.4's `open_plain` moves to `file::open_plain(root, rel)`, shared by the viewer (`file::read`, `read_plain`), `file::open_inside` (so `table::read` and the workbook reader), the repository's script files (`scripts::repo`) and, from the security review, a save's version check (`save_with`; a FIFO there blocked a runtime worker): on Unix `/` is opened, then each folder of the canonical root and of the checked path with `openat(O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW)` and the file with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, and the handle must be a regular file, so a link swapped in anywhere in the path, the worktree folder itself included, or a FIFO, is refused at once and the file is inside the worktree by construction; a worktree moved after the check is refused too (`scripts::repo` canonicalizes its folder first). From the same review: the worktree creation's check for a project's own `WorktreeCreate` hook (`worktree::own_create_hooks`) opens the settings files through `file::open_inside` (a FIFO there blocked the creation), and the untracked files' line count (`changes::count_lines`) opens with `O_NOFOLLOW` too. Links inside the worktree are still followed (the path is resolved first). Windows (`windows::open_parts`): `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` on the joined path, then the handle's metadata; a folder swapped for a junction is still followed there (`shortcut:` comment, D17.0). Tests (`file.rs`): through `opened_with` and `save_with`, which take the step between the check and the open, a file and a folder swapped for links out of the worktree, a FIFO answering at once (on a thread, 10 s bound), a worktree moved or swapped for a link, the relative-path and canonical-root checks; a FIFO settings file (`worktree.rs`), a link at the counted file (`changes.rs`).* Decided by the human at the Stage 23 checkpoint, 2026-11-06, from the security review of 23.6: between the path check and the open (`open_file`, `file::open_inside`, the table reader of `hive::table` and `hive::workbook`), an agent could swap the file for a symlink out of the worktree, or for a FIFO that blocks a worker thread. Open with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` and check the opened handle (a regular file, inside the worktree), reusing 23.4's `open_plain` in `scripts/repo.rs` (on Windows `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT`, then the handle's metadata). Tests: Rust, a link swapped in after the check is refused, a FIFO in place of the file answers at once without blocking.
- [x] **24.8 Measure the cost of clearing the groups' folder cache.** `task/24.8-settle-cost` — *Measured, negligible: no code change. Temp dirs only (`/var/tmp`), with a temporary instrumented build (not committed) counting `settle` calls, `scan` calls and their time; medians, the machine loaded by other agents' builds. (1) `settle` alone, one group of 1024 entries (the `GROUP_ENTRIES` cap), 5 rounds of 200 calls: median 1.09–1.11 ms with 300 repositories, 1.50–1.54 ms with 1024, ~1.0 ms for a folder of 5000 entries (read up to the cap); a cached lookup 72 µs. (2) A session through the real daemon (a group of 300 repositories and 700 folders, ~2 300 dirs; the group added, 5 worktrees added and removed in one of its repositories, 3 `list_projects`), 3 runs (one timed out on the loaded machine): `settle` ran once at start, once per registry wake (5–6 per run: the 10 changes came in debounced bursts) and once per `list_projects`, 8 in all; each cleared cache cost one scan, median 3.3 ms (1.9–6.7 ms). Why it does not matter: `list_projects` calls `forget` (which clears `scanned` too) before `settle`, so there `settle` adds no scan (`list` reuses its scan); a registry wake runs `settle` (one scan), then `worktrees_changed`, whose `forget` + `list` scans again and runs git on every repository: median 984 ms (709–1916 ms) for the 300, so the extra scan is ~0.3% of a wake, at most one per group per burst, never per request. A slow folder is read twice per wake instead of once; clearing only the changed groups would save that one read and nothing a user can see.* Decided by the human at the Stage 23 checkpoint, 2026-11-06: 23.1's `Projects::settle` clears the groups' folder cache (`scanned`) on every registry change and every `list_projects`, partly bringing back the cost 14.1 avoided for slow group folders. Measure first: a group on a large folder, how often `settle` runs (registry changes, `list_projects`), and the time spent scanning again; optimise only if the cost is real (e.g. clear only the groups whose folder changed). Report the numbers in this task's note.
- [x] **24.14 No Tauri IPC from a framed page, no invoke key in the log.** `task/24.14-frame-ipc` — *Tauri's `tracing` feature is on (the human, 2026-11-07): its refusal of a wrong invoke key, which names the right one, becomes a `tracing::error!` and the app has no subscriber, so nothing reaches stderr (`app.log`); `a_wrong_invoke_key_leaves_the_right_one_out_of_the_log` runs the refusal in a child test process and checks its stderr (it fails without the feature). Cost: a mock IPC round trip measured 540–600 ns with and without the feature (7 × 20 000 calls, release, the machine loaded), no difference above the noise; each call passes a dozen disabled spans, a few ns each. Frame gate, macOS: `macos::guard_ipc` replaces wry's `userContentController:didReceiveScriptMessage:` (class found by its name part `WryWebViewDelegate`: objc2 names it `wry::…::WryWebViewDelegate0.57.1`) and the `ipc` scheme handler's `webView:startURLSchemeTask:`; a message passes only from the main frame at an `ipc_from_app` URL (`app_url`'s origins), a fetch only with such an `Origin`. Checked in the real app (a debug build with 24.1's spike stub scheme, temp `HOME`, not committed): a sandboxed (`null`) and a plain `hive-file:` iframe both still see `webkit.messageHandlers.ipc`, their `postMessage` was dropped and their `fetch("ipc://…")` failed, the app's own 12 calls passed, and `app.log` had no `__TAURI_INVOKE_KEY__` line. Windows has no frame gate (D17.0).* Decided by the human at the 24.1 checkpoint, 2026-11-07, from the spike: a `sandbox="allow-scripts"` iframe (origin `null`) still reaches `webkit.messageHandlers.ipc`, Tauri treats a registered custom scheme as a local origin, the invoke key is the only barrier, and on a mismatch Tauri writes the expected key into `app.log`. Before 24.4: refuse IPC from any frame but the app's top document (and from the `hive-file` scheme), and keep the invoke key out of the log (filter or lower the level of the message that carries it). Tests: Rust where it can be driven (the origin/frame check, the log filter); Playwright or a manual check in the real app, written down in the report, that a framed page's IPC call is refused and the log holds no key.

- [x] **24.13 The space picks a GitHub account, as it picks a Claude account.** `task/24.13-space-gh-account` — *The space's account carries its folder: `SpaceEnv.gh_account` is `SpaceGhAccount {host, login, config_dir}` (`hive_protocol`, the login flattened), `gh_config_dir` gone from `SpaceEnv`; `spaces::gh_dir` gives it to `spaces::vars` (`GH_CONFIG_DIR`, terminals and Hive's git and `gh` runs), `Gh::vars`/`Gh::run` (the token looked up there) and the pull requests' cache key. The settings keep `gh.config_dirs` (`GhSettings`): checked as a space's folder (absolute, printable, ≤ 4096 bytes, trailing separators dropped), once each, at most 64, a new one must exist; `set_with` refuses to remove one a space's account is in (`Projects::using_gh`), naming the space. Migration: `Projects::load` takes each space's `env.gh_config_dir` out of the file's JSON (`legacy_gh`), puts it in its `gh_account` as `config_dir` (a folder that is no absolute path goes) and hands the distinct folders to `Store::migrate` with the 12.2 accounts (`migrate_accounts`: settings saved first, then `spaces.json` without the field; retried at the next start on a failure). App: the space dialog's folder field is gone; `useGhAccounts` (`SpaceDialog.tsx`) asks `list_gh_accounts` for `gh`'s own config and each settings folder (when shown and when the folders change), the store keeps one answer per folder (`ghAccounts` by folder), and the select lists every folder's accounts, an extra folder's named with its last segment ("work (gh-work)"); "Make active in gh…" and "Use the GitHub account's" run in the account's folder. Settings → Accounts has a "GitHub accounts" group in the Claude accounts' columns (one row per folder: logins, folder, a "Not logged in"/"Incomplete" badge with `gh`'s problem, remove; `~/.config/gh` first, never removed) and "Add a gh config folder" (`FolderField`, hidden folders, from home). Mock: each extra folder has one account, `mock-<folder>`. Tests: Rust (the migration in `projects` and through a daemon start in `accounts.rs`; the env of a terminal of a space whose account is in a folder, `spaces.rs` integration; settings checks, refusal and migration; `vars`, `using_gh`, `Gh::vars` in the folder); bun (no folder field; the select lists every folder's accounts and saves the folder; Settings adds, refuses and removes a folder); Playwright (`spaces.e2e.ts`: an account picked from an added folder). **Decided by the human, 2026-11-06:** the proposal below, confirmed as written.* *(Was 25.18.)* Asked by the human, 2026-11-06: the space dialog's "GitHub CLI config folder" field (`SpaceDialog.tsx:28`, `gh_config_dir`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR`) does not belong there; the space shows only the account select, as for the Claude account (22.5, `SpaceDialog.tsx:121`). **Proposal (the human confirms at the start):** the select lists every account logged in to `gh` (9.30's `list_gh_accounts`, logins only), from gh's own config and from each extra gh config folder kept in Settings, beside the Claude accounts (22.3): a "GitHub accounts" part listing them, with "Add a gh config folder" (the 22.2 `FolderField`) for a folder of its own; an account remembers its folder, and a space whose account comes from such a folder gets that `GH_CONFIG_DIR` in its terminals, as today. An existing space's `gh_config_dir` moves into Settings' folders when the settings load (no account lost), then leaves `SpaceEnv`. If #48's wording (the space's `GH_CONFIG_DIR`) needs a change, the human updates `docs/hive.md`. Tests: Rust, the migration of a space's folder into Settings and the env a space's terminal gets from its account's folder; bun, the space dialog has no folder field and its select lists the accounts of every folder, Settings adds and removes a folder; Playwright against the mock, picking an account from an extra folder.

## Stage 25 — The agent's browser, panes and previews

**Split from the old Stage 25 on 2026-11-08 at the human's request** ("split Stage 25 and what is still open into two stages; 25.16 goes in the one run first; weigh by each task's complexity, not their number"): the open tasks were sized with planning poker (Fibonacci points) and spread so each stage carries about the same work, dependencies kept in order (25.1 before 25.2's (5); 25.2 before 25.3, since it takes the Browser view out of the switcher). This stage ≈ 54 points (with 25.7, added the same day); Stage 26 ≈ 44, before 26.1 adds the SSH build tasks. Each moved task says its old number. Planned after Stage 24. New dependencies need the human's approval (rule 3) before `cargo add`.

- [x] **25.1 A file in a split, and changing what a pane shows.** `task/25.1-file-split` — *Any two tabs of a worktree are a pair: `Split` holds `PaneId`s (`number`: a terminal; `string`: a file's `fileKey`, and any later tab kind's key), `setSplit({left, right}, focus?)` is the one action that pairs. `shownPane` / `shownPanes` (`tabs.ts`) say what shows; the focused file pane is the open file (`focusFilePane` on a pointer down or focus in it), the other reads what its tab keeps through `paneState` (`FileTab.text`, `.table`, `.edit`, `.editing`), so `FileView` only gained `file`, `pane` and `onMenu`. `followOpenFile` / `followOpenTable` also ask for `asideFile`; `reduce` sends its answers to its tab. The drop listeners moved to `.terminal-body`, in capture, ahead of an editor. **Reproduced (2026-11-08) on the mock in Chromium and WebKit, on unchanged code:** dragging a *terminal* tab onto a pane with its highlight, the pane menu's and the tab menu's "Replace with ▸" and "Swap sides", a pair shown and hidden, all work. What fails, and why: (a) a *file* tab has no menu at all and its drag is refused, no highlight (`droppable` took terminals only, D20.4); (b) nothing can be dropped while a file shows (`droppable` needed `!fileShown`, and the listeners were on the hidden host); (c) a pair holding the worktree's only two tabs has "Replace with" disabled, with no reason given, the likeliest case with one agent and one shell; (d) its submenu listed every plain terminal under the same name. All fixed; no rule of 20.5 changed beyond the task. **Not exercised in the real app:** a debug build would run, but nothing here can send it native clicks or drags without taking over the human's pointer, so the drag in a real WKWebView is for the checkpoint (a tab onto a pane, its highlight, both menus). Tests: bun `src/split-files.test.tsx`; `split.e2e.ts`, a file dragged onto a terminal pane, a terminal replaced by a file through the menu, editing and saving in a pane, ⌘S on the focused one only.* *(Was 25.13, earlier 25.16.)* Asked by the human, 2026-11-06: "the split only happens between terminals, I cannot put a file split with an agent, and I cannot change what is inside the split". (1) **A file in a pair:** changes D20.4 (20.5: "files never enter a pair"): any two tabs of a worktree's bar can be paired, a file with a terminal or two files, with the pair rules of 20.5 unchanged (one pair per tab, shown together, joined in the bar, one divider). The file pane is the same file view (editor, its preview, Save, Diff), with its own focus; ⌘S and the editor's keys act on the focused pane only. A preview tab (11.1) that enters a pair is kept, as a double click keeps it, so opening another file does not replace a pane. "Split right" on a file tab pairs it with the worktree's next tab in no pair, else opens a terminal. (2) **Changing a pane:** 20.5 already has a tab dragged from the bar onto a pane and "Replace with ▸" in the tab and pane menus, but the human cannot do it: first reproduce in the real app (drag onto a pane, both menus, a pair shown and hidden) and say what fails and why; then fix it, and make the way findable (the pane's drop highlight on a drag, "Replace with ▸" listing the worktree's files too). Report what was found before changing the rules. Pairs are still not kept across a reload, and stay inside one worktree. Tests: bun, the store pairs a file and a terminal and two files, a paired preview is kept, "Replace with ▸" lists files, ⌘S saves only the focused file pane; Playwright against the mock (`split.e2e.ts`), a file dragged onto a terminal pane, a terminal replaced by a file through the menu, editing and saving the file pane.
- [x] **25.2 The agent's browser in a tab of its own.** `task/25.2-browser-tab` — *The service sends `browser_action {worktree, agent, agent_title, action}` for each `mcp__hive-browser__*` `PreToolUse` of an agent while its worktree's browser runs (`browser::caption`: plain text, each quote 120 characters, the whole 300), `browsing` in `agent_state` until it stops, `browser_status` for every worktree (with the page's `title`), and keeps each stopped browser's URL, title and frame until the app reloads; a browser stopped because its agents ended first gives one `Page.captureScreenshot`, so the result shows though the tab was never in view. In the app `BrowserTab` joins `BarItem` and `PaneId` (`browserKey`, `paneBrowser`), `shownBrowser` and `showBrowserTab` / `closeBrowserTab` / `showBesideAgent` (`store.ts`) drive it, `BrowserView` moved from the panel to the tab, and the agent's row has its globe. `VIEWS` has no Browser view any more, for 25.3. The dev-dependency `tokio` gains `test-util` (paused time in tests; no new package).* *(Was 25.16.)* Asked by the human, 2026-11-08, after watching a simulated agent drive 24.6's browser (a script playing the MCP client through `hive browser-mcp`, never the real `claude`): "I expected a new tab, as when I open a file". Seen then: nothing said a browser had started; the panel's Browser tab was cut off at the bar's edge (25.3); a 1280-wide frame in the ~380px panel is about 30% of its size, unreadable; the view did not say which agent did what; and the browser stops with the worktree's last agent, so its last page is gone when the human wants to check the result. Changes 24.6's place for it (the right panel's Browser view) and what is left once it stops. **The human's choice (2026-11-08), the agent's recommendation as written:** (1) **A tab in the worktree's bar**, as a file's (8.21): a globe icon, the page's title as its label, the URL as its tooltip; at most one per worktree, as the browser. It opens by itself on the worktree's first browser tool call (a `PreToolUse` whose `tool_name` starts with `mcp__hive-browser__`), at the bar's end and **without the focus**; closed by the human, it does not come back by itself while that browser runs (only by (2) or the palette). The screencast still runs only while the tab is shown and the window visible (24.6). (2) **A sign on the agent:** the agent's row in the sidebar shows a globe while it uses the browser (from its last browser tool call until it stops), pulsing once at each action; a click on it opens and shows the tab. (3) **In the tab:** a bar with the URL and "Open in my browser" (the system's default browser, the URL only; the view stays view only); a caption of the last action, from the hooks' `tool_input` (e.g. "<agent> · clicked 'Learn more'", "typed 'Honey bee' and pressed Enter", "opened https://…"), naming the agent that made it; the frame scaled to fit the tab's width, never above its size. (4) **After the browser stops**, the tab stays with the last frame, the URL and "Browser closed" until the human closes it; a new browser of the worktree takes the same tab. (5) **Beside the agent:** once 25.1 lets any tab into a pair, the tab's menu has "Show beside the agent", pairing it with the terminal of the agent of the last action; never done by itself. (6) The right panel's Browser view goes (`VIEWS`, `RightPanel.tsx`), which 25.3 then no longer has to fit. Later, only if the human asks: a timeline of the actions with a still frame each, and a picker when the agent opens several pages (today the last opened or navigated one shows). Not kept across a reload, as pairs. Tests: Rust, the browser tool calls mapped to the worktree's browser and its agent, the caption from each tool's input (untrusted, size-capped), the last frame kept after the browser stops; bun, the tab opens unfocused on the first call and not again once closed, the sidebar's globe and its click, the caption, the closed state, no Browser view in the panel, the screencast only while the tab is shown; Playwright on the mock transport, an agent's browser calls open the tab, the frame fits the tab, the closed tab stays closed. Needs 25.1 for (5) only.

- [x] **25.4 Edit a file in its preview.** `task/25.4-preview-edit` — *Markdown: `src/viewer/blockEdit.tsx`, a click on a `[data-line]` block (not a link, button or the end of a selection) hides it and shows a text area with lines `data-line` to the new `data-line-end`; each keystroke reaches the buffer (`withBlock`: only those lines of the `Text` change, CRLF kept) while the preview keeps its text until Escape or a click outside. CSV / TSV: a new service request `edit_table {worktree, path, content, edit}` answered by `table_edited {content, sheet, error}` (`hive::table_edit`: `scan` finds each cell's bytes by the `csv` reader's rules, a test holding them to it, and an edit is splices on them, so every other byte stays; quotes only where needed; the file's first line ending; `""` for a row left with one empty cell; nothing on disk). `TableView` gained a selected cell (click, arrows), a cell editor (double click or Enter; ⇧Enter a line, Tab next, Escape drops), a context menu for rows and columns and "Add a row" on an empty file; a sheet not whole is read-only with a note. `tableAsks` / `followTableEdits` send the edits one at a time; `saveOpenFile` (now `src/viewer/save.ts`) waits for them. A `table` from disk is ignored while dirty, and the table asks from the buffer when it shows again, so the text view can go back to it. `BufferNotes` shows the conflict banner and a save's error over both previews. 24.12's replace row is not wired: it needs a batch of edits and CodeMirror's replacement rules for cells, not a small addition; the find bar stays find only. Tests: Rust `table_edit.rs` (the scan against the reader, byte for byte, each quoting case, TSV, line endings, partial and over-the-cap refused) and integration `file.rs`; bun `blockEdit.test.tsx`, `TableView.test.tsx`, `RightPanel.markdown.test.tsx`, `RightPanel.table.test.tsx`, `follow.test.ts`, `mock.test.ts`; Playwright `preview-edit.e2e.ts`.* *(Was 25.12, earlier 25.14.)* Asked by the human, 2026-11-06 (screenshot: `example.csv` in its table view): a text file shown as a preview can be edited there, not only in the text. Only text formats: Markdown and CSV / TSV today (HTML once 24.4 gives it a preview); never a PDF, a workbook (23.7) or any binary file, which stay read-only. Choices of the human (2026-11-06): (1) **Markdown, a block becomes its source:** clicking a top-level block of the preview (paragraph, heading, list, table, code block; the blocks 21.3 marks with `data-line`) swaps that block alone for its Markdown in a small editor; Escape or a click outside renders it again; everything outside the edited block stays byte for byte as it was; no new dependency. (2) **CSV / TSV, cells and rows:** a double click or Enter edits a cell; rows and columns can be added and removed; only when the whole file was read (the sheet not `partial`, 23.6's 10 000 rows, no row over the limit), else the table stays read-only with a note pointing to the text. Writing the file back is the service's (#37): it keeps the file's delimiter, line ending and quoting style (quotes only where a cell needs them, RFC 4180). Both save as the text does: the same tab, the same dirty mark and Save (⌘S), through `save_file` with the version check (#6), and the text view shows the edits when toggled back. Tests: Rust, a CSV edit written back keeps an unedited file byte for byte and quotes a cell with a comma, a quote or a line break, a TSV keeps its tabs, a partial table refuses an edit; bun, a Markdown block opens its source and only that block's lines change, a cell edit and an added / removed row mark the tab dirty and save; Playwright against the mock, editing a block and a cell, then Save.
- [x] **25.5 Claude Code's Computer Use in a Hive terminal.** *(Was 25.14, earlier 25.19.)* Asked by the human, 2026-11-06 (screenshot: `claude` in a Hive terminal, "Computer Use needs macOS permissions", Accessibility granted, Screen Recording not granted, "Open System Settings → Screen Recording" / "Try again"). A program in Hive's terminals gets macOS privacy permissions through its responsible app (D18.4, 18.20: Hive.app), so Hive itself must hold Screen Recording (and Accessibility) for Computer Use to work. **The human's report (2026-11-06):** with the computer-use MCP, Hive was allowed when asked and it still does not work. Seen on this Mac then (read-only): `hive-app` (pid parent launchd) → `~/.hive/bin/hive bridge` → `hive daemon`, all started together, so a terminal's process descends from Hive.app; Hive.app is ad-hoc signed (`com.lucas.hive`, no team); the TCC database could not be read (no Full Disk Access). The human then confirmed the grant is for this build and Hive was restarted. **Checked by the agent (2026-11-06):** Claude Code 2.1.311 (`/opt/homebrew/bin/claude`, a Homebrew cask) does Computer Use in its own process (an embedded native module, `computer-use-swift.node`, calling `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` / `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess`), not in a helper app; `responsibility_get_pid_responsible_for_pid` gives Hive.app (`/Applications/Hive.app/Contents/MacOS/hive-app`) as the responsible process of that `claude`, of the daemon and of a shell in a Hive terminal, so the MCP's check is Hive's (the third cause is ruled out). Yet a probe built with `cc` in a Hive terminal, after Hive's restart (19:40), answers Screen Recording 0 and Accessibility 1. Hive.app's signature is valid (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), ad-hoc with the hardened runtime, and its designated requirement is only its `cdhash`; `tccd`'s log showed nothing readable. Left to find: why the grant does not match this `cdhash` (a stale entry System Settings shows as on, or Screen Recording refusing an ad-hoc identity), e.g. by `tccutil reset ScreenCapture com.lucas.hive`, granting again from the prompt Hive's terminal raises, and checking with the probe after a restart. First find out, on this Mac, with a small test program in a Hive terminal (never the real `claude`; e.g. a `cc` build calling `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` / `AXIsProcessTrusted` and printing the result): (1) which app TCC holds responsible for a terminal's process (Hive.app, or the `hive` daemon / bridge binary, which is started by the bridge and may outlive the app), and so what must be listed in System Settings → Privacy & Security → Screen & System Audio Recording; (2) whether a grant works once Hive is restarted, and whether the daemon must restart too (its terminals would close); (3) whether the grant survives an update under ad-hoc signing (D17.1 (a): every update changes the code identity, so it is likely lost). **D17.1 decided (the human, 2026-11-06): a self-signed certificate**, so Hive supports this without the human configuring things again on each update ("I will not keep configuring countless things; Hive should support this"). macOS never lets an app grant itself a permission: the human allows once; the work is that this one grant works and lasts. (a) **A stable identity:** a self-signed code-signing certificate (made once by the human, its `.p12` and password as CI secrets; the agent writes the steps in the README) signs every release's Hive.app in `release.yml` instead of ad-hoc, with the hardened runtime as today, so the designated requirement names the certificate, not a `cdhash`, and TCC grants (Screen Recording, Accessibility, folders) survive updates; `macos-bundle.sh check` verifies the signature is that certificate's. The first build signed this way changes the identity once: the release notes say to allow Hive one last time. Local dev builds stay ad-hoc. (b) **Hive shows it:** Settings lists, for programs in Hive's terminals, Screen Recording and Accessibility as granted / not granted (read by the app with `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` / `AXIsProcessTrusted`, never prompting on its own), each with a button opening its System Settings pane (`x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_ScreenCapture` / `?Privacy_Accessibility`) and a note that Hive needs a restart after granting Screen Recording. Then make it work and say it plainly: whatever the app needs (`Info.plist` keys, the responsible process being Hive.app and not an unsigned binary, `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess` only if a prompt is needed), and a README section "Computer Use and screen recording" with the steps (add Hive, restart it, what an update does). Report the findings before changing code. Tests: Rust or a script for whatever code changes (e.g. the `Info.plist` keys checked by `macos-bundle.sh check`); the permission itself is a hand check by the human, listed in this task's note. *Branch `task/25.5-computer-use`. **Done:** (a) `release.yml` signs the app and its `hive` with the certificate when `HIVE_CERTIFICATE*` exist, else ad-hoc with a `::warning::`; `macos-bundle.sh` gained the `self-signed` mode, `new-certificate`, `import-certificate` (CI only), `check --self-signed <SHA-1>` and `release-notes`; a real identity signs a bundle in `test-scripts.sh` on a CI runner only (`codesign` needs the keychain on the search list). The human restricts the `release` environment to `v*` tags, then creates the certificate, after this merge (README, "Signing with Hive's certificate"). (b) Settings → Terminal → macOS permissions, `check_terminal_permissions` / `open_privacy_settings`. **Findings:** (1) already answered above: Hive.app. (2) from the code: the daemon cannot outlive the app (it lives as long as the app connection), so restarting Hive restarts it and closes its terminals; there is no case of "Hive restarted, daemon not". Whether a grant takes effect before the restart is the hand check. (3) from the designated requirement: ad-hoc names a `cdhash`, so every build is another identity to TCC and the grant is lost; a certificate's names the identifier and the certificate, so it survives; the hand check confirms it. **Hand checks (the human, at the checkpoint; nothing here was run):** in a Hive terminal, `cc probe.c -o probe -framework CoreGraphics -framework ApplicationServices`, with `probe.c`: `#include <ApplicationServices/ApplicationServices.h>`, `#include <CoreGraphics/CoreGraphics.h>`, `#include <stdio.h>`, `int main(int argc, char **argv) { if (argc > 1) CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess(); printf("screen=%d accessibility=%d\n", CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess(), AXIsProcessTrusted()); return 0; }`. Then: `./probe` (reported: `screen=0 accessibility=1`); `tccutil reset ScreenCapture com.lucas.hive`; `./probe request` and allow Hive; `./probe` again (before the restart: does a grant need one?); restart Hive and `./probe` in a new terminal (expected `screen=1`); Settings → Terminal shows the same (this also checks `main.rs`'s wiring, `with_terminal_permissions` and the two commands, which no gate can see: without it the group silently disappears). After the first certificate release: `codesign -d -r- /Applications/Hive.app` names the certificate; allow Hive; update to the next release; `./probe` still answers `screen=1`.*

- [x] **25.7 Opening Hive's own `TODO.md` freezes the app.** Seen by the human, 2026-11-08: opening this repository's `TODO.md` in Hive takes about 5 s and the whole app freezes lightly meanwhile (not only the file view); other projects' Markdown files open at once, and the human does not know what is particular about this one. What is known: it is about 241 KB, about 460 lines, many of them 2–5 KB long (task lines), and it changes often while agents work. First **find the cause, with numbers, before changing code**: reproduce on the mock transport or a dev build with a copy of the file (and a generated one: same size with short lines, and long lines in a small file) to tell size from line length; profile where the time goes (the service reading and sending the file, the message size, the frontend's Markdown preview or CodeMirror, the outline, links or search, a re-render on each file change) and why the whole window stalls (work on the main thread, a blocking call). Then fix the cause where it lives (#37: Rust for reading and sending, the frontend for rendering), so the file opens with no visible stall and the rest of the app stays responsive while it loads; if the fix needs a human decision (e.g. a limit, or rendering the preview in parts), stop and ask. Report the before/after numbers. Touches the same file view as 25.4: whichever runs second merges with the other. Tests: a regression test on the cause found (e.g. Rust, a large file read within a bound; bun, the preview of a 250 KB file with 5 KB lines rendered within a time bound or in parts), with numbers in this task's note. *(`task/25.7-large-markdown`. **Cause:** the editor's change markers (`gutterMarkers.ts`) called `Chunk.build` with no limit, a Myers diff of characters, quadratic in the changed characters; on the webview's one JS thread, so everything in the window waited. Not the size: 259 KB of short lines with 30 rewritten took 99 ms, 50 KB with two 5 KB lines rewritten 658 ms, 240 KB with five 8571 ms; long lines make every change thousands of characters. It needs a base that differs, which is why other files open at once, and it ran again at each new text from disk (`load` builds a new state). The Diff view has its own `scanLimit`, the text and preview no diff. **Numbers, before → after** (`TODO.md`, the base one commit back; production bundle, Playwright WebKit, median of 3): `Chunk.build` alone on bun 5462 → 2.7 ms, the same 32 marks (`git diff`: +29 -27); opening in the editor 15232 → 94 ms, longest stall 15163 → 51 ms; a new text from disk while open 8377 → 43 ms. Base 40 commits back: over 180 s, never finished → 108 ms. **Not the cause:** the service, 40-80 ms from asking to the answer in a debug app and service on a temporary `HOME` against 21-28 ms for a tiny file (487 KB of JSON); the preview 130-370 ms in one task, the read-only text and Diff about 150 ms, unchanged. **Tests:** `gutterMarkers.test.ts`: the chunks hold whole lines (not timing: a one-character edit is a change of its whole line), the exact markers on 250 KB of 5 KB lines (with a 2 s backstop: a few ms now, the characters' diff takes 2-4 s on that pair), that the editor builds and updates with the line diff, the fallback on a collision of ids, and both scan limits by their results. The human's 5 s was not reproduced in a visible real window: the debug app's window was hidden in the runs of the editor view, which throttles its timers, so those numbers are WebKit's.)*

## Stage 26 — Remote connections over SSH

Created 2026-11-05 at the human's request: "I want something for remote connections, for now only over SSH, as Orca has; I want my agent to think about the best way to bring this". Planned after Stage 25 (split from the old Stage 25 on 2026-11-08; each moved task says its old number). **Changes `docs/hive.md`** (Hive today runs its service on this machine or in WSL, #1, #26, #29). The design was chosen on 2026-11-09 (26.1, `docs/spike/ssh.md`); the human updates `docs/hive.md` from its section 13. Tasks 26.13–26.21 build it. - [x] **26.1 Design: Hive over SSH.** `task/26.1-ssh-design` — *Design picked by the human (2026-11-09): [`docs/spike/ssh.md`](docs/spike/ssh.md). The whole service runs on the remote, reached by the stdio bridge over the user's own `ssh` (`BatchMode=yes`, `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, a constant remote command running `~/.hive/bin/hive bridge`). The doc also holds the comparison with stablyai/orca, the rejected alternatives, the measurements and the security notes. Echo over ssh is 0.4 ms at p50 on loopback and 102.7 ms with 100 ms RTT; a plain `ssh -tt` is no faster. The lab was a local `sshd` with temp keys and a delaying proxy, since Docker was not running. The human's answers:*
  - *A space belongs to one host.*
  - *Remote agents keep running after a dropped link, and after quit unless the user says otherwise (keep is the default).*
  - *The keep time is 24 h by default, 1 minute to 7 days.*
  - *Static Linux x86_64 and aarch64 builds are bundled in the macOS app and uploaded over ssh.*
  - ***Linux hosts only**: macOS and everything else is refused with a message.*
  - *An unknown or changed host key is refused ("run `ssh <alias>` once"), and Hive never writes `known_hosts`.*
  - *Keepalive 10 s × 3, plus a reconnect on wake.*
  - *A remote service of another version holding terminals: both versions shown, restart it or stay disconnected.*
  - *Screen restore is a ring-buffer replay plus a forced redraw.*
  - *The 512 KiB window does not change.*
  - *Remote Linux terminals use `$SHELL` as a login shell.*
  - *Hive does not force `ControlMaster`.*
  
  *Defaults the answers leave open: ledger rows 26.1 (1)–(4).* *(Was 25.1.)* No production code; a throwaway branch for experiments only, never the human's real hosts (a local `sshd` in a container or a temp user, with a temp `HOME` and keys). The agent **thinks through the best way** and presents it at the checkpoint, with a recommendation:
  - **What Orca does** (its docs and source): how a remote is added, what runs on the remote, what the local app keeps, how it reconnects, what it does not support. Say what to copy and what not, and why.
  - **The shape that fits Hive.** Hive already splits app and service and already reaches a service elsewhere through a stdio bridge: on Windows the app runs `wsl.exe --exec … hive bridge` (`bridge.rs`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), and the service owns the PTYs, git, hooks and files (#37). The obvious candidate is the same bridge over `ssh <host> -- ~/.hive/bin/hive bridge` (22.1's folder), with the service, its terminals, worktrees, hooks and the `claude` wrapper all on the remote; compare it with the alternatives (the service local and only PTYs or git over SSH; SSH port forwarding of the socket; an SFTP or `sshfs` file layer) on latency, what keeps working when the link drops, and how much code changes.
  - **One app, several services:** today the app talks to one service. Decide whether a remote is a separate window, a space (#48) bound to a host, or projects of any space on any host, and what the sidebar, status bar, notifications (#5), session history and the files panel show for a remote project; local and remote at the same time is the expected case.
  - **Installing and updating the remote service:** the remote's OS and CPU (Linux x86_64 and arm64 first; macOS hosts too?), where the `hive` binary comes from (the release's builds, copied over SSH by the app as the bridge copies it into WSL, #29, and the versions handshake), what the remote needs (git, `claude` installed and logged in on the remote: Hive never copies credentials, #45).
  - **The link:** the user's own `ssh` and `~/.ssh/config` (aliases, `ProxyJump`, keys, agent), never a password or key stored by Hive, separate arguments only, host keys always checked (an unknown or changed key asks or refuses, never `StrictHostKeyChecking=no`); dropped links and reconnects (the remote service keeps its terminals running, as `tmux` would; what the app shows meanwhile); laptop sleep; `ControlMaster` reuse.
  - **What is out for now**, said plainly (e.g. Windows hosts, password prompts, a remote with no shell).
  - **Risks and security** for `security-reviewer`: what a malicious or compromised remote could send the app, and the limits that already apply to the service's messages.
  - **The plan:** the tasks 26.11… to build it, each sized like the other stages' tasks, written into this stage only after the human approves.

  Deliver `docs/spike/ssh.md` (design, measurements of a terminal's echo latency over a local and a delayed link, e.g. `tc netem` or a proxy with 100 ms, and the open questions). ⏸ Checkpoint: the human picks the design; the agent then writes 26.11… here and the human updates `docs/hive.md`.

Needs every 12.5 sub-task.
  *Security review follow-ups (`task/12.5.7-followups`, human decisions 2026-09-29): a path from the app (folder browser, `add_project`, Claude and GitHub CLI config folders, a terminal's `cwd`) must start with a drive (`windows::local`: `Prefix::Disk`/`VerbatimDisk`; UNC, verbatim UNC, device and verbatim paths refused before any file call, "… network and device paths are not supported: use a drive path such as C:\…"); the service's pipe is `\\.\pipe\hive-<SID>-<random>`, its name in the runtime folder's `hive.sock` (at most 256 bytes, only a hive pipe's name; a stale one fails at once and the bridge starts a new service); "Copy Resume Command" is the service's `Session::resume_command`, written for the terminals' shell (moved from `src/sessions.ts`).*
  *Residuals (`task/12-residuals`): a link to a network or device path is never followed on Windows: `paths::canonical` (every resolution of a file the service opens, reads, saves or lists, and each link the folder browser lists) first walks the path with `windows::local_links` (each name read without following it, each link's target by `read_link`, `.`/`..` taken off by name as Windows does, at most 63 links), refusing a target that is not relative, rooted, on a drive or on a mounted volume ("<link> points to a network or device path, which Hive does not open"); the watcher tells a folder by `symlink_metadata`. `sessions::powershell` is the only PowerShell quoting (the statusline command uses it).*
  *macOS test flakes (`task/12-macos-flakes`): no wrong-process kill. A terminal's shell (always the second of two opened together) died of SIGTRAP before `exec`: the crash report shows macOS's `fork` aborting the child in libnotify's atfork handler ("os_once_t is corrupt") when another service thread was setting libnotify up at that moment; `hive daemon` now sets it up (`notify_is_valid_token(0)`) before its runtime starts any thread. The echo-latency and timeout failures were load: every test terminal's fish, in a fresh cache, ran Python over every man page to generate completions (~1 core each); `common::Env` now gives it an `XDG_CACHE_HOME` where they count as generated (no bound changed). Stress (`cargo llvm-cov -p hive` on macos-14): 6 failing runs in 100 before (3 SIGTRAP shells, 2 latency), 1 in 50 after (a 10 s wait for git to open the FIFO in `terminals_echo_while_agents_are_being_placed`, no crash, not yet explained).*

  - **Pending defaults for the ledger:** clicking the selected icon of an open view does nothing (not asked to close it; the agent reports it as an option); whether the view reopens by itself after a restart follows what `rightPanel` does today.
  - **Tests:** unit and e2e for each case above, at the panel's widths, in light and dark; the specs that used the removed button (`e2e/shell.e2e.ts`, `shortcuts`, `panel-tabs` and the others a search turns up) move to the X, the rail or the shortcut with no assertion lost. `docs/ui-reference.md` and a new ledger row replace 25.3's tab order (rail, Collapse, view).
  - **Hand check at the checkpoint:** in the real app, the X leaves the rail, each icon reopens its view, Ctrl+Shift+B does the same, and the terminal bar has no panel button.

### Hive over SSH (from 26.1)

The design is [`docs/spike/ssh.md`](docs/spike/ssh.md) with the human's answers of 2026-11-09 (its "Decisions" section; ledger 26.1 for the rest):

- the whole service runs on a remote Linux host, reached by `ssh` and the stdio bridge;
- one link per host;
- a space belongs to one host;
- a remote's terminals outlive a dropped link and are reattached.

Every task that changes `crates/hive` or `src-tauri` gets `security-reviewer`. Tests never use the human's hosts or `~/.ssh`: only temp keys, a temp `HOME` and a local or containerized `sshd`.

- [x] **26.13 Linux builds for remote hosts.** `task/26.13-linux-remote-builds` — *`.github/workflows/linux-remote.yml` (reusable; called by `ci.yml` on every code change, by `macos.yml` before `bundle`, by `release.yml` before `macos`) builds `hive` for `x86_64`/`aarch64-unknown-linux-musl` with `cargo zigbuild` (cargo-zigbuild 0.23.4 from taiki-e/install-action, Zig 0.16.0 from ziglang.org checked by its SHA-256), each on a runner of its own CPU (`ubuntu-latest`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`), then `scripts/linux-smoke.sh <hive> <app version>` runs it in `debian:10` (glibc 2.28): `--version`, then `hive daemon` with a temporary HOME and runtime folder listens (a Perl connection, closed without a hello) and ends with 0 on SIGTERM, its socket gone. Artifacts `hive-linux-<arch>`; `macos.yml` and `release.yml` put them in `src-tauri/resources` (mode restored) and `tauri.release.macos.conf.json` bundles them. `macos-bundle.sh check` verifies both: executable, a 64-bit little-endian ELF of the right `e_machine`, no `PT_INTERP` (static; musl's `static-pie` counts), a `PT_LOAD`, and the app's `CFBundleShortVersionString` in the binary (a Mac cannot run an ELF: the smoke test is what runs `--version`). They are not codesigned: the app's signature seals them as resources, which `check`'s `codesign --verify --deep --strict` covers (a test changes one after signing). `file::rename_new` on Linux calls the `renameat2` system call itself: nix wraps it only for glibc, and Zig's musl has no such symbol. Measured locally (Zig in a container): x86_64 5.9 MB, aarch64 5.2 MB, both statically linked; both pass the smoke test in debian:10 (x86_64 under emulation). Not verified until the next real release: the Developer ID signing and notarization of an app holding ELF files, and the release job itself.* Spike §6.2, Q4, Q5. Needs nothing.
  - **Builds:** static musl `hive` for `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` and `aarch64-unknown-linux-musl`, built by a new `release.yml` job and checked (build only) in `ci.yml`.
  - **Build tool:** `cargo zigbuild` or `cross`. The agent proposes one and waits for the human's approval before installing it (rule 3).
  - **Bundled:** the macOS app carries them as `hive-linux-x86_64` and `hive-linux-aarch64`. WSL keeps today's glibc `hive`.
  - **`macos-bundle.sh check`** verifies that both are there, both are static, and both print the app's version.
  - **Smoke test in CI:** each build runs in an old-glibc container (e.g. `debian:10`; aarch64 under QEMU or on an arm runner). `hive --version`, then `hive daemon` with a temp `HOME` starts, listens, and ends on SIGTERM.
  - **Report:** the app's size before and after.
  - **Tests:** `scripts/test-scripts.sh` for any script added or changed.
- [x] **26.14 Remote Linux terminals use the login shell.** `task/26.14-linux-login-shell` — *`hive bridge --remote` starts `hive daemon --remote`, which sets `terminal::set_remote` before anything starts a shell (Linux only; macOS and Windows accept the flag and change nothing). macOS's `shell` and `path_shell` moved out of `macos.rs` into portable Unix code: `terminal::login_shell` (terminals) and `wrapper::login_shell` (the service's `PATH`, `$SHELL -l -c`), used on macOS and by a remote Linux service; WSL keeps `fish -C` / `fish -c`. On Linux `login::usable` keeps `$SHELL` only when it can run (an absolute path to an executable file, or a name found as one on the service's absolute `PATH` folders), and the terminal starts the absolute path it found; else `login::DEFAULT_SHELL`: `/bin/sh` on Linux, `/bin/zsh` on macOS as before. `sh` and `dash` also get Hive's `ENV` file `<data>/hive/shell/shrc`, which puts the bin dir first again after the login profile (a `~/.profile` that prepends `~/.local/bin` would otherwise put another `claude` first). Hive's startup files (`<data>/hive/shell`) are now written on Linux too, unused on WSL. Integration tests in `tests/integration/remote.rs` (Linux) for bash, zsh, fish, no `$SHELL` and a missing one; `setup-rust` installs zsh. Defaults in the ledger.* Q11. Needs nothing.
  - **Remote mode:** `hive bridge --remote` starts `hive daemon --remote`. WSL and native Windows are unchanged (WSL keeps `fish -C`, #25).
  - **Terminals:** a remote service runs each terminal as the user's `$SHELL` login shell, with Hive's bin dir first once its login files ran, as `terminal::login` does on macOS (the macOS code made portable Unix code).
  - **The service's own `PATH`** (for `git`, `gh`, `claude -p` and the wrapper's `real_claude`) comes from that login shell, as `macos.rs` asks it.
  - **No usable `$SHELL`** (missing, or not executable): `/bin/sh -l`.
  - **Tests:** integration tests (`common::Env`) for bash, zsh and fish (CI's Linux job installs the missing ones) and for a missing `$SHELL`:
    - the wrapper's `claude` is first on a terminal's `PATH`;
    - a `git` found only on the login shell's `PATH` is the one the service runs.
- [x] **26.15 A remote service outlives a dropped link.** `task/26.15-remote-keep` — *`hello {keep_secs, app_id}` (optional, left out when absent: the local app's JSON is unchanged) and `bye {end}`. `daemon::serve` keeps a deadline after a kept drop (`Gone::Keep`), checked again when it fires, so an app back meanwhile cancels it. `terminal::Output` holds the app's queue and, for a terminal opened by a `keep_secs` connection, a `hive::ring::Ring` (1 MiB, filled attached or not, with `u64` offsets); `Output::detach` on a kept drop (or a takeover) wakes a pump waiting for acknowledgements and leaves it writing only into the ring. For 26.16: `Output::attach(frames)`, `Output::kept(|ring| …)`, `Ring::{start, end, since}`. The ring's last 1 MiB is checked in `terminal.rs`'s unit tests (no message reads it before 26.16's `attach_terminal`). Defaults in the ledger.* Q2, Q3, spike §6.3. Needs nothing.
  - **Protocol 1, optional fields:** `hello` gains `keep_secs` and `app_id`; a new `bye {end}`. A connection without `keep_secs`, and `bye {end: true}`, keep #18 exactly as today.
  - **When an app connection with `keep_secs` ends without `bye {end: true}`:**
    - the service keeps every terminal and chat for `keep_secs`;
    - each terminal's output goes into a 1 MiB ring buffer (the oldest bytes dropped). The PTY is **never paused** while no app is attached; 9.19's window applies again once one is;
    - hooks, states, transcripts and worktrees are processed as now;
    - after `keep_secs` with no app, the service ends as it does today (`open-sessions.json` written).
  - **Takeover:** a new app connection with the same `app_id` replaces a stale one, and the old connection is closed. Another `app_id` while one is attached keeps today's refusal.
  - **Docs:** `docs/architecture.md` (Handshake, App disconnect, message catalog).
  - **Tests:**
    - integration: the socket is dropped mid-flood and the program keeps writing past 512 KiB with no stall;
    - integration: the terminals end after a short test grace time;
    - integration: the takeover, the refusal for another `app_id`, the ring keeping the last 1 MiB, and `bye {end: true}` ending at once;
    - protocol: round trips with and without the new fields.
- [x] **26.16 Reattach after a dropped link.** `task/26.16-reattach` — *Protocol: `terminals_kept {terminals: [KeptTerminal {channel, cwd, worktree, account, offset}]}` (service → app, channel 0, first after `welcome` when a terminal has a ring; `State::terminals_kept`, places kept in `State::kept`), `attach_terminal {from}` (app → service, on the terminal's channel; `State::attach` → `terminal::Output::reattach`, which replaces 26.15's `attach`) and `terminal_reset {offset}` (service → app, the ring's start, before the whole ring). App (`src-tauri`): `Term::received` counts each terminal's bytes; `Link::kept` answers `terminals_kept`: a terminal with a UI output gets `attach_terminal {from: received}` and the UI `terminal_reattached {channel}`; one it does not have becomes a `Term` with no output and the UI's 18.21 `terminal_kept`, whose `attach_terminal` command then sends `attach_terminal {from}` instead of the `ack`; `terminal_reset` writes RIS (`CLEAR_SCREEN`) into the terminal's output. UI: `redrawTerminal` / `reattachTerminal` resize one column wider and back. For 26.17: keep the `Term`s across a dropped link and the reattach follows from `terminals_kept`. Defaults in the ledger.* Q9, spike §6.4. Needs 26.15.
  - **Offsets:** every terminal counts its output in bytes (a `u64` offset); the app counts what its xterm received.
  - **After `welcome`:** a service holding kept terminals sends `terminals_kept [{channel, cwd, worktree, account, offset}]`. The app answers `attach_terminal {channel, from}`.
  - **Replay:** a `from` still inside the ring gets only the missing bytes. Otherwise the service sends `terminal_reset`; the app clears that xterm and gets the whole ring.
  - **Redraw:** the app then resizes by one column and back, so a full-screen program (Claude Code) redraws.
  - **Unknown terminals** (the app restarted) open as tabs under their worktree, from the ring.
  - **Chats (#40):** the agent does the same for a kept chat's stream, or reports it as a gap.
  - **Docs:** `docs/architecture.md`.
  - **Tests:**
    - Rust: an offset inside, at the edge of and outside the ring, and the reset;
    - bun: the xterm is cleared and replayed, the redraw resize is sent, unknown terminals open as tabs;
    - integration: detach, 2 MiB written, reattach, every byte that is still in the ring arrives in order.
- [x] **26.17 The app's links over ssh.** `task/26.17-ssh-links` — *`src-tauri/src/remote.rs`: `Remotes` follows the local service's `remote` settings (`Link::follow` on `settings`), one supervised link per alias (`supervise`: connect, install, ladder, wait for Reconnect or wake). `Hive` derefs to the local link's `Host`; `Hive::on(host)` routes the request commands (each takes an optional `host`), the terminal commands find their link by id; ids are shared by every link (`Link::ids`), and a remote's messages carry `host`. `start`/`speak` split out of `spawn`/`attach` for both kinds of link. The upload's stdin starts with `<bytes> <sha256>`, checked on the host with `sha256sum` before the file runs (the 26.13 review). New: `host_link` (app → UI), `reconnect_host` and `restart_remote` commands, `version_mismatch.terminals`, `hive bridge --replace` (the lockfile holds its holder's pid), `macos::observe_wake`. After 26.16, a link welcomed again reattaches its kept terminals (`Link::kept`: `attach_terminal {from: received}` and `terminal_reattached`; `terminal_kept` with a new id, `Link::kept_id`, for one the app lacks) and ends those `terminals_kept` does not list (`Link::unconfirmed`/`confirmed`); a local `version_mismatch` now ends its connection's terminals and state too (`refused`, 26.16's review). Tested with a fake `ssh` (`src-tauri/tests/fixtures/fake_ssh.c`: argv log, stderr replay, `/bin/sh` as sshd, or a pipe to a test service) and the real scripts under `/bin/sh`; no real sshd was needed. Defaults in the ledger.* Spike §6.1–6.2, Q4–Q8, Q12. Needs 26.13 and 26.15; reattach on reconnect uses 26.16.
  - **One link per host** in `src-tauri`: the local service and each remote. UI messages and app commands carry `host`; terminal channels are mapped per link so ids never clash.
  - **The command:** `ssh -T -o BatchMode=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes -o ClearAllForwardings=yes -o ServerAliveInterval=10 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o ConnectTimeout=15 -- <alias> <constant>`.
    - There is no `ControlMaster` option: the user's config decides.
    - The alias is validated: no leading `-`, no whitespace or control characters, at most 255 bytes.
  - **Check and install:**
    - the spike's constant check-and-run command, then the upload command with 26.13's build for the remote's `uname -m`: byte count and `--version` checked before the rename;
    - `Darwin`, or any OS other than Linux or any CPU other than x86_64 and aarch64, is refused with its message (Q5); the `uname` text is cut to 64 characters and shown as text.
  - **Handshake:** `hello` carries `keep_secs` (the setting `remote.keep_minutes`, default 1440, range 1–10080) and `app_id`, on remote links only.
  - **stderr classified, each case with its message:**
    - unknown or changed host key: "run `ssh <alias>` once in a terminal to check and accept its key". Hive never writes `known_hosts`;
    - authentication: a passphrase or password was asked, so "add the key to your ssh agent";
    - network;
    - unsupported platform;
    - another version (Q8): both versions shown, with "Restart the remote service (ends N terminals)" or "Stay disconnected". Restart runs a new `hive bridge --replace`, which ends the running service (SIGTERM to the lockfile's holder) and starts its own.
  - **Reconnect:** a ladder of 1, 2, 5, 10 and 30 s, then every 30 s while the host has kept terminals. No retry after a host key, authentication, platform or version error. An immediate try on wake (`NSWorkspaceDidWakeNotification`).
  - **Settings:** the hosts list (`remote.hosts`) and `remote.keep_minutes` live in the local service's settings.
  - **Tests:**
    - a fake `ssh` (a C fixture like `fake_claude.c`) that checks its argv, replays each stderr case, or pipes to a test service;
    - the ladder with a fake clock;
    - the alias validation;
    - the install's checks;
    - channel mapping across two links.
