## Stage 27 and after — Ideas from Zed's Delta

Created 2026-11-24 at the human's request, after a study of Zed's Delta (a multiplayer app for coding with agents, public beta 2026-09-16; https://zed.dev/blog/introducing-delta, https://delta.dev/docs): "do 3 ao 7, quero que organize meu sistema para conseguir prover funcionalidades semelhantes", i.e. the study's groups 3 (review instead of the pull request), 4 (real-time collaboration), 5 (cloud, web and phone), 6 (worktrees and infrastructure) and 7 (interface details). Hive stays an observer (#52): Delta's own agent harness, its subagent profiles and the agent committing, pushing or opening pull requests by itself are **not** copied. The human's answers (2026-11-24):

- **D27.1** Collaboration comes **over SSH**: teammates reach the same remote service of Stage 26 with their own app; no Hive server, no Hive account, no network of Hive's own (#14 holds: the link is the user's own `ssh`).
- **D27.2** Cloud runners (Delta's hosted sandboxes) are **out for now**. A remote Linux host of the user's (Stage 26) already keeps agents running with the laptop closed.
- **D27.3** Commit, push, create pull request and run CI become **actions the user clicks** in Hive. **This changes 9.31 (b)** ("Hive never pushes"); the agent is still only observed (#52) and nothing merges by itself (#12). The human updates the ledger and `docs/hive.md`.
- **D27.4** The plan lives here as stages, each opening with a design task and a ⏸ checkpoint where the work is new; no code before the human approves each design.

Not taken from Delta, and why: a terminal inside a conversation (Hive has no conversation view since 9.2/9.35); the Claude Code sync plugin (Hive already sees Claude Code through its hooks, #21); a web or phone client (needs a server or an open port, against #14; D27.1 brings collaboration through the app only). Order: Stage 27 first; the rest of these ideas moved into Stages 28–29 when the stages were rebalanced on 2026-11-27 (old 30.x → 28.6–28.8, old 29.x → 28.9–28.13, old 28.1 → 28.14 and its build 29.4). New dependencies need the human's approval (rule 3) before they are added.

## Stage 27 — Review before the pull request

Today: a read-only diff against HEAD or the branch's fork point (9.11), live changes (3.1–3.2), gutter markers (14.4), review comments typed into the terminal without Enter (6.7), GitHub pull requests and Actions through `gh` (9.31, 9.32).

- [x] **27.1 Files marked "seen" in the diff.** `task/27.1-seen-files` — *`mark_seen {path, base, files, seen}` → `changes`, whose files carry `seen` (`seen` / `changed`, left out when unmarked); `hive::seen` (identity = blob ids of both sides, hashed only for marked files) over `hive::worktree_store` (`<data>/seen.json`, 1 MiB, `0600`, atomic; reusable by 27.3); marks dropped by `remove_worktree` / `remove_project` and, for a folder gone, at the next save. UI: the box at the right end of each Diff row and of the diff's header, the dot, "k of N seen" with "Unseen only", the palette's "Mark all as seen"; `e2e/seen.e2e.ts`. Defaults: ledger 27.1 (1)–(3).* Delta's Changes tab has a "seen" box per file and a count ("4 of 9 seen"). The service keeps, per worktree and diff base, each file's seen mark with the hash of the diff it was marked on (`git hash-object` of the patch text, or the blob ids of both sides); a file whose diff changes after it was marked goes back to unseen, with a dot saying "changed since seen". Marks survive a restart (a small JSON file in the Hive folder, 22.1, size-capped; no write inside `.git`, #56) and go when the worktree is removed. UI: a box on each file row of the Changes panel and on the diff's file header, the count in the panel's bar, a filter "unseen only", the palette command "Mark all as seen". Tests: Rust, marks kept, invalidated by a new change, dropped with the worktree, the file capped; bun, the box, the count, the filter; e2e on the mock transport.
- [x] **27.2 More diff bases: the agent's last turn and a chosen commit.** `task/27.2-diff-bases`, `task/27.2-diff-bases-impl` — *Done: design in `docs/spike/diff-bases.md`; built as its 27.2a–c (`hive::snapshot`, `DiffBase::Turn`/`Commit`, `list_commits`/`commits`, the base menu and `CommitPicker`), see the Defaults ledger. The human's answers (2026-11-26): Q1 option B (Hive's own bare store in `~/.hive/snapshots/<repo>/` with `objects/info/alternates` to the repository's objects, refs `refs/turns/<session>/<n>`, Hive's own prune) fits #56 read as "no file created, moved or changed in `.git`"; the mtime refresh of objects git meets again is accepted; a user's `git gc` removing a snapshot's blob makes the base fall back to HEAD with `base_error`. Q2 the last 10 turns per session, at most 7 days, dropped at `SessionEnd`. Q3 1 GiB per repository, oldest first. Q4 large untracked files are included. Q5 no snapshot for a subagent in its own worktree. Q6 a snapshot that finished after the agent's first edit is shown with a warning. Q7 500 commits, the branch's first. Q8 listing through the warm snapshot index. Q9 filters, the repository's hooks (`core.hooksPath` = an empty folder) and the split index off for snapshot git calls.* Delta compares against an upstream ref, the last commit, the last agent turn or a chosen commit. Today Hive has HEAD and the fork point (9.11). **Design first** (⏸ checkpoint): how the service snapshots a worktree at the start of each agent turn (the `UserPromptSubmit` hook, #21) without touching the user's index or branches, e.g. a temporary index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE` in Hive's folder, `git add -A`, `git write-tree`) kept as a tree id per session with untracked files included and ignored ones left out; where the tree ids live, how many are kept, the cost on a large repository (measure it), and whether this crosses #56 ("never write inside `.git`": git's own object store) — the human decides that point. Then: the diff's base picker gains "Since the agent's last turn" (per agent of the worktree) and "A commit…" (a searchable list of the branch's commits). Tests: Rust, a snapshot per turn, the diff since it, the user's index untouched, the cap; integration with the fake `claude` (hooks fired); bun, the picker; e2e.

- [x] **27.4 Review in a separate worktree.** `task/27.4-review-worktree` — *`hive::review`: `create_review` makes the Claude worktree `review-<name>` on a new branch of that name from `refs/heads/<branch>` (`worktree::create_on`; `-2`, `-3`… when taken; refused when detached); the link is kept in `<data>/hive/reviews.json` (0600, ≤ 256 links), marked on every listing as `Worktree::review_of`, dropped when git lists a project without either worktree, moved by a rename. "Bring changes back…" (`plan_bring_back`, `bring_back {path, tip}`) shows the commits and merges only on the click: `merge --ff-only <tip>` when the reviewed branch did not move, else `merge --no-ff` with a message and the space's identity, never a rebase or cherry-pick; refused with changes to tracked files on either side or a plan that no longer holds (`into`, `base`, `tip` sent back); a conflict is aborted, a refusal of git's own left alone, a merge Hive killed undone (`merge --abort` / `reset --merge`), and git's stdout and stderr shown (`git::ran` keeps both; last 64 KiB). `security-reviewer` run: its medium (a rollback over staged changes) and lows (into/base not checked, unbounded message, reads without a time limit) fixed. "Discard review…" (`discard_review`) is 4.4's removal, then the `review-…` branch goes when every commit of it is on the reviewed branch. The sidebar nests a review under its worktree with a "review" badge. Defaults in the ledger.* — Delta gives a reviewer an isolated copy of the work, to try and fix things without touching the original. In Hive: "Review in a new worktree…" on a worktree's menu creates a worktree on a new branch `review-<name>` from the reviewed branch's tip (git refuses the same branch twice), shown under the reviewed one and marked as its review; its terminals and agents are ordinary (#52). When done: "Bring changes back" asks, shows the commits that would go, then merges or cherry-picks them into the reviewed branch **on the user's click only** (#12), refusing with uncommitted changes on either side and stopping on a conflict with git's message; "Discard review" removes it (4.4's checks). Tests: Rust, create, bring back (fast-forward, merge, conflict refused), discard; integration; bun, the menu and dialogs; e2e.
- [x] **27.5 From a line to the session that wrote it.** `task/27.5-line-provenance-impl` — *Design `docs/spike/line-provenance.md`, approved by the human 2026-11-26 with every recommendation: Q1 one shared `anchor` module (line hashes, bounded diff, range mapping, relocation by text and context nearest the old line), code shared only, each feature its own store (27.3 reuses it); Q2 a hand-written bounded Myers over hashes, no new dependency; Q3 lines from Bash or another editor show "unknown" with the reason; Q4 "at that edit" = the session's row selected in the Sessions view with its "Files changed" open and the file highlighted, a session of another worktree under "From <worktree>"; Q5 a `Write` without Claude's patch is diffed against the stored map, else every line is the session's; Q6 caps 2 MiB / 50 000 lines per file, 20 000 records and 8 MiB per repository, 90 days, 64 MiB in all (ledger `pending`); Q7 records outlive a deleted session ("session deleted"); Q8 no shortcut; Q9 `hive hook` reads up to 32 MiB for `PostToolUse` and slims it to the fields Hive uses. Built: `hive::anchor`, `hive::slim` (`hive_edit` line hashes; the file text never crosses the socket), `hive::provenance` (records, line maps, re-sync at each edit / tool end / turn end / editor save / lookup, `who_wrote` live else `git blame` + the records before the commit, renames through `git diff-tree -M`, `session_files`, `files_changed` on `Session`, the store in `<Hive folder>/provenance/`), protocol `who_wrote`/`line_author`, `list_session_files`/`session_files`; the app's gutter menu (editor and both diff sides), the palette command, the answer popover, the Sessions row's files and "From <worktree>". Defaults in the ledger.* Delta links any line to the conversation that wrote it. **Design first** (⏸ checkpoint): the service already sees each `PostToolUse` of `Edit`, `MultiEdit` and `Write` with the session id and file (#21); decide what to record (file, the new text's hash and line range, session, time), how lines are followed as the file changes (as 27.3) and after a commit (`git blame`'s commit → the sessions that edited those lines before it), the size cap, and that payloads are untrusted and never logged in full. Then: "Who wrote this?" on the editor's and the diff's line menu opens the session in the Sessions view (4.11) at that edit, and the session's row lists the files it changed. (2) **Push:** "Push" on the worktree's menu and in the panel, to the branch's upstream or, with none, `origin/<branch>` with `--set-upstream`; never `--force` (no option for it), a rejected push shows git's message. Credentials are git's and `gh`'s (9.30), never stored by Hive. (3) **Create pull request** (9.31) offers to push first when the branch is not on GitHub. (4) **Run CI:** in the Actions view, "Run workflow…" for workflows with `workflow_dispatch` (`gh workflow run`, the branch and inputs asked), then the run followed as 9.32 does. `security-reviewer` on the new git and `gh` calls. - [x] **27.7 A subagent Claude Code already closed stays listed.** `task/27.7-stale-subagent` — *Done: the stale line was a subagent failed on an API error (`StopFailure` with its `agent_id`, no `SubagentStop`); it now leaves on its failure (`Agent::fail`), waits if a launch of its own may wake it, and a teammate stays; diagnosed with synthetic payloads (`fixtures/failed_subagent_synthetic.json`), see the Defaults ledger.* Asked by the human, 2026-11-25 (two screenshots): an agent's row ("error · 10h · 4 background") lists **5** subagents, one of them `subagent` in error (`XCircle`) since 8h; Claude Code's own list for the same session shows only the **4** `general-purpose` ones still running, so it closed the fifth long ago, but Hive keeps it. Expected: a subagent leaves Hive's list once Claude Code no longer has it, error or not. **Diagnose first** (`superpowers:systematic-debugging`): reproduce with the fake `claude` and recorded hooks (26.2's spike) which events a subagent that fails sends and which it does not; today (`crates/hive/src/states.rs`, `apply`/`prune`/`leave`) a subagent leaves only on its `SubagentStop` (or later, once its launches are gone from `background_tasks`), its `SessionEnd` or its worktree's `WorktreeRemove`, so a likely cause is a failed subagent that gets an error state (`StopFailure`/`TurnFailed` with its `agent_id`) but never a `SubagentStop`. Then fix it from what Claude Code reports, never from a timer alone: e.g. a failed subagent leaves on its failure, and/or on each `Stop`/`SubagentStop` the listed subagents are reconciled against the session's `background_tasks` (or the transcript, `session_watch.rs`) and those Claude Code no longer runs leave; teammates (26.10) and waiting subagents (5.10) keep their rules. The agent's own state is not changed by it. Tests: Rust, states.rs (a subagent that fails without `SubagentStop` leaves; one still in `background_tasks` stays; a teammate stays), the recorded sequence replayed in integration; bun only if the row's rendering changes.

## Stages 28–33 — Toward Hive 1.0.0

**Rebalanced 2026-11-27 at the human's request** ("o stage 27 está sendo feito agora, quero que organize os demais stages, utilize um planning poker para definir uma estimativa simples para cada tarefa, daí divida tudo em 5 stages com cargas semelhantes, obviamente analisando o que depende do quê"): every open task of the old Stages 28–36 was sized by a planning poker of three voters (a service/Rust engineer, a frontend engineer and a QA/delivery lead), Fibonacci points, the median taken (the votes differed by at most one card). A task whose content waits for a design carries a reserved budget the voters agreed on. About 484 points in all, spread over five stages of about the same load, dependencies kept in order (a task never needs one of a later stage). Each moved task says its old number; decision ids (`D27.N`, `D31.N`, `D34.N`) kept theirs. Stage 27 is in progress and was not touched.

| Stage | Content | Points |
|---|---|---|
| 28 | The usage fix, skills, performance baselines, interface details, worktrees and tooling, and the designs the later stages need, and the interface fixes (28.17–28.21) | 103 |
| 29 | Pinned tabs, and working together over SSH | 98 (89 reserved) |
| 30 | IDE features in the editor, themes from a file, the plugin design | 90 (34 reserved) |
| 31 | Plugins, and the tooling audit | 95 (89 reserved) |
| 32 | The full review of Hive's code (audits and approved refactors) | 100 (55 reserved) |
| 33 | The design pass: every detail of UI and UX, on weekends with Fable, then Hive 1.0.0 | 149 (133 reserved) |

Why this order: the usage ring is broken for the user today (first); the skills (28.3–28.4) help every later task, and the performance baselines (28.5) must exist before language servers and plugins add their cost; the keymap (28.7) gives go to definition (30.3) and plugin commands their bindings; the colour-blind diff colours (28.6) feed the theme format (28.16); the multiplayer design (28.14) settles where pins are kept (29.1) and the protocol before new messages pile up; the code-intelligence and theme designs (28.15, 28.16) come before the plugin design (30.7), since plugins contribute grammars and themes; the audits run last (Stage 32) so they review the code 1.0 ships, and the release closes it.

Created 2026-11-27 at the human's request: "quero algumas funcionalidades que tem em IDEs, como por exemplo syntax highlight, quero ver onde as variáveis estão sendo usadas e quantas vezes assim como no VS Code, além de outras coisas; além disso, quero melhorar a customização do Hive, disponibilizar algumas formas de plugins, por exemplo, highlights para algumas linguagens, alguma feature nova, integração com alguma coisa, temas; no caso dos temas acho que algo para configurar no arquivo de configuração como cores, espaçamentos, fontes, arredondamento etc. já seja interessante, mas mudar um botão de lugar precisa de um plugin customizável; depois que essas tarefas forem finalizadas quero lançar a versão 1.0.0 do Hive".

- **D34.1** Code intelligence in the editor (28.15, Stage 30) **changes #6** ("sem LSP na v1") if the approved design uses language servers: the human confirms it at 28.15's checkpoint and updates `docs/hive.md`. Whatever analyses code runs in the service (#37); the frontend only paints what it receives.
- **D34.2** **Themes are configuration, not plugins** (the human, 2026-11-27): colours, spacing, fonts, corner radius and the like are set in a theme file. Changing the interface itself (e.g. moving a button) is a plugin's job.
- **D34.3** **Plugins** may add, at least: highlighting for more languages, a new feature, an integration with an outside tool or service, and themes (the human's examples, 2026-11-27). What a plugin can reach and how it runs is 30.7's design, approved by the human.
- **D34.4** **1.0.0 comes after the IDE features and the plugins** (the human, 2026-11-27; Stages 30 and 31 since the rebalance). Each new area opens with a design task and a ⏸ checkpoint; no code before the human approves each design (as D27.4).

## Stage 28 — Fixes, foundations and designs

≈ 103 points. Three designs end here at ⏸ checkpoints (28.14–28.16), so the human approves them while this stage's other tasks run.

Created 2026-11-26 at the human's request: "o painel para mostrar o uso de plano não está mais funcionando, preciso que isso seja ajustado". In v0.30.0 the status bar shows the space's account ("Gmail - Visus") and the version, but no usage ring (12.1, 21.8, 22.5); the menu bar icon's usage lines (17.22) and the usage alerts (17.23) depend on the same reports. A bug fix: it restores the existing rules and changes no decision. *(From the old Stage 33.)*

Facts seen on the human's Mac on 2026-11-26, to start from (not a diagnosis): `~/.hive/usage.json` holds only the `gmail-visus` account, its 5-hour window past its reset (`resets_at` 10:33 -03 that day), so no report seems to have arrived since that morning; both accounts' `settings.json` (`~/.hive/accounts/*/settings.json`) set their own `statusLine` (`~/.claude/statusline.sh`), while `~/.hive/hive-hooks.json` sets `hive statusline`; which one Claude Code runs, and whether the current Claude Code still sends `rate_limits` in the statusline input, is unchecked.

Worktrees and tooling, from the old Stage 29:

- [ ] **28.9 Copy-on-write for `.worktreeinclude` files.** · 5 pts *(Was 29.1.)* `task/28.9-cow-copy` — Delta makes cheap copies with APFS/Btrfs/XFS clones. Hive keeps git worktrees (Claude's convention, #15), but the files `.worktreeinclude` lists (`node_modules`, `.env`, build caches) are copied byte by byte (4.7). Copy them with a clone where the filesystem allows (macOS `clonefile`, Linux `FICLONE` ioctl), falling back to a plain copy; links never followed out of the project. Measure a large `node_modules` before and after in the report. Tests: Rust, the clone and the fallback behind a trait, the same files copied either way; integration on a temp APFS disk image on macOS.
- [x] **28.10 JSON output and read commands in the `hive` CLI.** · 5 pts *(Was 29.2.)* `task/28.10-cli-json` — *Done: the human approved the proposal on 2026-11-27 (they update `docs/hive.md`), so no checkpoint before coding. `hive worktree list --json` prints `{"worktrees": [{path, branch, bare, prunable}]}`; a read-only `hive agents [--json]` asks the service on a hook-role connection (`list_agents`, answered by one `agent_listed` per agent in terminal order, then `agents_listed`; the service copies the agents before writing, so a client that does not read never holds the agents lock) and prints each agent's terminal, state, worktree, activity, title and context, or `{"agents": [...]}` (`ListedAgent`). No service running: a message on stderr and exit code 3 (1 for any other failure). Shapes in `docs/architecture.md` (CLI output); `hive::query`.* Delta's CLI answers with `--json`. **Changes a revoked decision** (the CLI's `status`, `esperar` and `resumo` were revoked; "the CLI never creates, starts or orchestrates agents" stays): the human confirms before it is built. Proposal: `--json` on `hive worktree list`, and a read-only `hive agents [--json]` (each agent's worktree, state, activity, context), for scripts and status bars; nothing that starts, stops or talks to an agent. Tests: Rust, the JSON shapes, no service running; integration.

The agent **thinks through the best way** and presents it at the checkpoint, with a recommendation:
  - **Who is who:** each teammate's ssh login on the host (one Unix user each, sharing the project folders through a group, or one shared user with a per-app name); what the service trusts as an identity, and what a teammate can and cannot see (spaces, accounts and credentials of others are never shown, #45).
  - **Several apps on one service:** what each app receives (the same projects, worktrees, agents and states), the protocol changes, the limits per link (26.19's caps per app), and what happens when one app drops.
  - **Terminals:** Delta lets only the one who opened a terminal type in it, others watch; decide the same or a "hand over" action; the PTY size when apps differ (the smallest, or the typing one's).
  - **Presence and follow:** who is connected, which worktree and tab each one looks at, "follow <name>".
  - **Shared review:** 27.1's seen marks per person, 27.3's comments shared with their author, `@name` mentions reaching the named one's inbox (6.5) and notifications (#5).
  - **Security:** a teammate's app as untrusted as a remote (26.19); no new port (#14).
  - **The plan:** the build tasks, written as 29.4… in Stage 29 only after the human approves; `docs/hive.md` updated by the human.

## Stage 29 — Pinned tabs, and working together over SSH

≈ 98 points, 89 of them reserved for the multiplayer build that 28.14's design will define. D27.1. Today a remote service serves **one** app: a second one with another `app_id` is refused ("another app is already connected", `docs/spike/ssh.md` §6; "two laptops on one host is out for now").

Created 2026-11-26 at the human's request: "quero conseguir fixar um chat para evitar que feche acidentalmente um chat".   - **What a plugin can add:** language grammars and highlighting, themes, commands in the palette (6.3), panels and tabs, status bar items, changes to the layout (moving or hiding a button, D34.2), integrations with outside tools or services.
  - **How it runs:** WebAssembly in the service (logic in Rust's process, #37) and/or UI code in the app, sandboxed; what each side may call; how a plugin that hangs or crashes is stopped without taking terminals with it.
  - **Permissions:** files, network, processes, the agent's data; each asked to the user by name at install and shown in Settings; a plugin as untrusted as a remote (26.19).
  - **Install and updates:** a folder of the Hive config, a file, or a Git URL; whether a catalogue is wanted (a Hive server would go against #14).
  - **A stable API for 1.0:** versioning, what breaks a plugin, how a plugin is tested.
  - **Decisions it changes** (#14, #37, others), marked "changes #N".
  - **The plan:** tasks written as 31.2… in Stage 31 only after the human approves, including at least two example plugins built on the API (e.g. a language's highlighting and an integration) to prove it; `docs/hive.md` updated by the human.

## Stage 32 — A full review of Hive's code

≈ 100 points, 55 of them reserved for the refactors 32.7 approves. Hive 1.0.0 moved to the end of Stage 33.

Created 2026-11-25 at the human's request: a complete review of Hive, mainly of its code — what can be improved or refactored while every rule keeps working the same way, possible optimizations, UX/UI adjustments — and a search for skills that improve development (frontend, security, etc.). Placed last at the human's request; its skills (28.3–28.4), performance baselines (28.5) and tooling audit (31.1) run earlier (rebalanced 2026-11-27).

- **D31.1** **Audit first, then refactor** (the human, 2026-11-25). 28.5, 31.1 and 32.1–32.6 are read-only: each writes a report and changes no product code. Refactor tasks are written only after the human approves the findings at 32.7's checkpoint.
- **D31.2** **Same behaviour.** A refactor or optimization keeps every rule of `docs/hive.md` and every existing test as it is: no test deleted, loosened or rewritten to fit the new code (rule 6), coverage stays at 100% and no mutant survives. A finding that needs a test's assertion changed is a behaviour change and goes to the human as such.
- **D31.3** **Skills live in the project** (`.claude/skills/`, the human, 2026-11-25), versioned with the repo so every `/stage` agent has them. Each one is approved by the human before it goes in, as a dependency is (rule 3).
- **D31.4** **UX/UI proposals may go beyond the prototype** (the human, 2026-11-25): a finding may propose a change to `docs/prototype/` or to a decision of `docs/hive.md`, marked "changes #N"; it goes in only with the human's approval, and the human updates `docs/hive.md` (agents never edit it).

**Report format (28.5, 31.1, 32.1–32.6):** one file per area in `docs/review/<area>.md`. Each finding has an id (`R<task>.<n>`, e.g. `R32.1.4`), where it is (`file:line`), what is wrong or could be better, why it matters (evidence: a measurement, a duplicated block, a bug it invites), the proposed change, the risk, the effort (S/M/L), whether behaviour stays the same (yes / "changes #N") and a priority (P1 must, P2 should, P3 nice to have). No finding without evidence from the code or a measurement; style preferences that `cargo fmt`, clippy and biome already settle are left out.

- [ ] **32.1 Audit: the service's core.** · 8 pts *(Was 31.3.)* `task/32.1-audit-core` — Needs 28.4 (the agent uses the approved skills). `crates/hive-protocol` and the daemon, terminals and PTYs, the ring buffer, hooks, agent states, sessions, settings and the socket: module boundaries and size, duplicated logic, error handling (no `unwrap`/`expect` outside tests, errors that lose context), locking and async (held locks across awaits, blocking calls on the runtime, unbounded channels or buffers), allocations and copies on hot paths (terminal output, hook events), dead code (`cargo machete`, unused items, the codebase-memory graph's dead-code query), protocol messages that could be smaller or fewer. Deliver `docs/review/service-core.md`.
- [ ] **32.2 Audit: the service's git, files and remote parts.** · 8 pts *(Was 31.4.)* `task/32.2-audit-git` — Needs 28.4. Worktrees, `git.rs` and the checkout, changes and diff, file watching and saving, search, `gh` and pull requests, scripts, tables, projects and spaces, the SSH remote (Stage 26): the same questions as 32.1, plus how often git and `gh` are spawned (count the processes per refresh on a repo with many worktrees), watchers that fire more than needed, and caching that would remove repeated work without serving stale data. Deliver `docs/review/service-git.md`.
- [ ] **32.3 Audit: the Tauri app.** · 3 pts *(Was 31.5.)* `task/32.3-audit-app` — Needs 28.4. `src-tauri/`: commands and capabilities, the custom scheme, downloads, the tray and menus, `macos.rs`, logging; the same questions as 32.1, plus startup time and what the app does on the main thread. Deliver `docs/review/app.md`.
- [ ] **32.4 Audit: the frontend's code.** · 8 pts *(Was 31.6.)* `task/32.4-audit-frontend` — Needs 28.4. `src/`: the store and `reduce.ts`, the transport, the components under `ui/`, `viewer/`, `shell/`: logic that belongs in the service (#37), duplicated components and helpers, components too large to follow, state kept twice, re-renders (React profiler on the mock transport with many worktrees, agents and terminals), effects without cleanup, listeners and timers that leak, the bundle (size per chunk, what could load lazily), TypeScript `any`s and casts. Deliver `docs/review/frontend.md`.
- [ ] **32.5 Audit: security.** · 8 pts *(Was 31.8.)* `task/32.5-audit-security` — Needs 28.4. A whole-repo pass (not only a diff) with the `security-reviewer` agent and the approved security skill: every trust boundary (hook payloads, socket messages, file names, environment, spawned processes, the SSH remote, the custom scheme, the agent's browser), size limits, file modes (`0600`), what reaches the logs, Tauri's capabilities and CSP, `cargo deny` advisories and the frontend's dependencies (`bun audit` or equivalent). Deliver `docs/review/security.md`; anything P1 is also told to the human at once, not only at the checkpoint.
- **32.6 Audit: UX and UI** *(was 31.9)* moved to Stage 33, which does it in full and fixes what it finds (the human, 2026-11-27).
- [ ] **32.7 Consolidate the findings.** · 3 pts *(Was 31.11.)* `main` — Needs 28.5, 31.1 and 32.1–32.5. `docs/review/README.md`: every finding in one table, sorted by priority, duplicates merged, and the refactor tasks proposed from them (each with the findings it closes, the expected gain, its risk and its dependencies), grouped in waves that can run in parallel, with "changes #N" proposals listed apart for the human's decision. ⏸ Checkpoint: the human approves which findings become tasks; the approved ones are written here as 32.8 (D31.1).
- [ ] **32.8 The refactor tasks approved at 32.7.** · 55 pts reserved *(Was "31.12…".)* — Written here after 32.7's checkpoint, in waves that can run in parallel (D31.1, D31.2).

## Stage 33 — The design pass, then Hive 1.0.0

The last stage, and the largest. Created 2026-11-27 at the human's request: "no último stage, quero que seja feito uma revisão completa do design do sistema, UI e UX, provavelmente esse vai ser o maior stage que vamos ter, quero que ele faça uma busca completa, ele pode perder o tempo que quiser, esse stage só pode rodar durante o final de semana usando o modelo Fable no alto, ele deve fazer ajustes nos mínimos detalhes do sistema, não deve ser apenas uma revisão e otimização de código, obviamente quero isso também". Needs Stages 28–32 finished (it polishes what 1.0 ships, after the code review's refactors).

- **D33.1 Weekends only.** The stage runs only on Saturdays and Sundays (the human's time zone, -03). The orchestrator starts or resumes its tasks only then; on Monday 00:00 every running task commits what is done, pushes its branch and stops; the next weekend resumes it. Merges into `main` follow the usual rules (CI green, reviews), any day.
- **D33.2 Fable at high effort.** Every agent of the stage, orchestrator, task agents and reviewers alike, runs on Fable (`claude-fable-5-2`; the Agent tool's `model: "fable"`) with effort `high`.
- **D33.3 No time limit.** Thoroughness over speed: the stage may take as many weekends as it needs. Every screen, state and flow is looked at, not a sample.
- **D33.4 Fixes, down to the smallest detail.** Not only a report: each pass fixes what it finds in the same task (spacing, alignment, sizes, colours, contrast, icons, wording, focus, motion, empty and error states, long names, keyboard), with the screenshots before and after. Only a change that moves a decision of `docs/hive.md` or a pattern of `docs/prototype/` is proposed first, marked "changes #N", and waits for the human (D31.4); the human updates `docs/hive.md`.
- **D33.5 Code too.** Each pass also reviews and optimizes the code it touches (D31.2: same behaviour, every test kept, coverage 100%, no surviving mutant), on top of Stage 32's audits.

**How each pass works:** the app on the mock transport through Playwright, in One Dark and One Light, at the smallest supported window, a usual one and a large one, with the states that matter (empty, loading, error, one item, many items, very long names, a remote host, a narrow panel); keyboard only and with a screen reader's labels; `prefers-reduced-motion`. Findings and fixes are listed in `docs/review/design/<area>.md` with before/after screenshots in `docs/review/design/<area>/`.

- [ ] **33.1 Inventory and the design system.** · 13 pts `task/33.1-design-inventory` — Every screen, dialog, menu, panel, state and flow of the app listed in `docs/review/design/inventory.md` with a screenshot of each; the design tokens (spacing scale, type sizes and weights, colours and their contrast, corner radius, borders, shadows, icon sizes, motion durations) measured against what the code actually uses: every off-scale value found and fixed, every duplicated component or style unified. ⏸ Checkpoint: the human sees the inventory and the token fixes before the area passes.

- [ ] **33.3 Pass: tabs, terminals and splits.** · 13 pts reserved `task/33.3-design-terminals` — The tab bar, chats and terminals, splits and their focus, drag and drop, exited and kept terminals, the terminal's font, cursor and selection.
- [ ] **33.4 Pass: the editor, the diff and reviews.** · 21 pts reserved `task/33.4-design-editor` — The editor and its gutter, Markdown and previews, tables, the diff and its bases, seen marks, comments, "Who wrote this line?", commit and push.

Each friction fixed or proposed.

## Not now (Fase 2)

Kanban screen, rich interactions (permissions and choices answered in the app), live edit view, session history. Do not build any of it during v1.

Also not now (the human, 2026-11-24, D27.2): hosted cloud runners and a web or phone client.

## Open items for the human

- **17.10: verify an N → N+1 update on a clean macOS user** (needs two published releases, N and N+1, and a Mac; agents cannot do it). (1) System Settings → Users & Groups → Add User (standard), log in as it. (2) In Safari download Hive vN's `.dmg` from GitHub Releases (so it is quarantined), open it, drag Hive to Applications, open it from Applications; on the block click Done, then Privacy & Security → Open Anyway, confirm. (3) No "Move Hive to Applications" dialog appears, and in Terminal `ps -o comm= -p $(pgrep -x hive-app)` starts with `/Applications/Hive.app`. (4) `codesign --verify --deep --strict --verbose=2 /Applications/Hive.app` passes. (5) Once vN+1 is published, reopen Hive: "Restart to update to vN+1" shows; click it (with no agent running): Hive restarts by itself. (6) Settings → About shows vN+1; repeat the `codesign` check and also `codesign --verify --strict --verbose=2 /Applications/Hive.app/Contents/Resources/hive`: both pass; quit and reopen Hive from the Dock: no "damaged" or "cannot be opened" message, a terminal opens and its agent is detected. (7) Translocation: as another user (or after deleting `/Applications/Hive.app`), drag Hive from the downloaded `.dmg` to `~/Downloads` instead and open it there: "Move Hive to Applications" opens once; the update button (if offered) opens it instead of updating. Report any step that fails; a "damaged" message in (6) means the nested `hive` lost its signature (17.11).
- Export PNG screenshots of prototype screens 1a–1g into `docs/prototype/stage-1/` before Stage 1 (Ponto em aberto 13).
